*Kiran: Please carry stumps, tape,cones and crease tape.*

*Kiran: No drinking water available. Please bring water.*
3.⁠ ⁠⁠is there any covered area/bleachers. do we tents?

MMF has dugouts (but quite dirty, i dont think we can fix by this weekend) and bleachers (like Mt. Si high school).
FWS has picnic tables and a play area nearby but no shade - canopy/tent can be useful.

We have dedicated locked storage - u can leave ur stuff at the field for next week.

*Long term SYCL will get stumps/canopy's etc. just that too much is going on the last few weeks and couldnt focus on getting these.*

[4/8/25, 11:17:05 PM] Anthony Bonthu SYCL: *Important USA CRICKET Membership*

Dear managers
Every player should have a USA MEMBERSHIP ID. You need to renew every year.

SYCL has 150 credits. We want to pass on these credits to the SYCL players.

*New players*
New players must register with USAcricket.org/membership

When you register, select SYCL as league and select any club under sycl. (All these clubs belong to MLCA, if any academy needs a club, please let us know)
*do not pay membership fees*

As I mentioned earlier, SYCL can pay membership fees for 150 kids. FCFS.

*Please note, USA cricket membership is must for playing in the hub*

[4/24/25, 4:48:04 PM] Kiran Komaravolu SYCL: Dear Managers - A few of you have asked as to why SYCL is not providing grounds to the Hub games. I want to provide some clarification.

We have been running behind Hub since early Jan asking them to share details on hub date window and an approx team count. These requests were met with inaction.
Hub asked us to help only on April which is way too late. 
 
There are a few of things I want to highlight.

1. Grounds need to be booked many months in advance. 
2. Last 2 years SYCL was given enough notice and we were able to secure more grounds and run+non-hub in parallel. We also did the scheduling for hub games.
3. In 2025 due to lack of clarity from the hub and other academies, we didnt book enough grounds - in fact we lost our biggest ground (Mt. Si high School) to other sports.
4. It is impossible for SYCL to help the hub or anyone with 1-2 weeks of headsup.
5. It is also unfair to the kids not playing in hub (phase2/Women) to give up their gameslots to support hub.

[6/19/25, 8:56:37 PM] Shiv Sycl Payments: *Final Spring Season Team Payments*

Dear Managers,

Now that all matches for the spring season are complete and invoices have been processed, we have calculated the final payable amount per team.

As a refresher from our 2024 year-end financial disclosure meeting, we are implementing a new fixed-rate model this year. This approach is based on our best estimates from the past year and is designed to avoid the significant delays and variable costs of previous seasons, which often burdened managers until all bills were settled. So hopefully this is much easier to collect from your teams

Our estimated costs, includes
1. facility rentals maintenance & equipment 
2. registration & Insurance
3. portable restrooms 
4. awards, and the awards ceremony
I will follow-up with a detailed break down in our Q2 financial meeting soon. 

The estimates are based on a 12-member team:

* T20 & T30 Matches: $20 per player, per match
* T40 Matches: $25 per player, per match

Typically, the number of matches is consistent across a division. However, due to numerous interruptions this spring, some divisions could not play their full quota. Therefore, for this season only, we are calculating costs on a per-team basis rather than amortizing them across the entire division.

The final payable amount for your team for the spring season is as follows:
‎[6/19/25, 8:56:57 PM] Shiv Sycl Payments: ‎image omitted
[6/19/25, 9:03:21 PM] Shiv Sycl Payments: As a non-profit member, everyone of us, the parents has the right and responsibility to seek the utmost transparency from SYCL and it will be my pleasure and duty to present it to you. So please do ask any questions that you may have. I will also go over the details in the Q2 Quarterly financial meeting by the end of the month. ‎<This message was edited>

[6/19/25, 10:25:15 PM] Shiv Sycl Payments: Yes, that is correct. $20/player/match is a guideline target for SYCL to hit (lowering from $23 last year). Another way for a team to think about it is $240 / team as the team is registered as a whole, not individual players (like hub for example). [6/20/25, 8:07:57 AM] Siddhi SYCL Cyclones Manager: For future reference only:  Rained-out/abandoned would they be usually be rescheduled ? Supernovas and Cyclones first match was washed and we barely played first few overs of first innings. For future I am wondering if such matches should be rescheduled? Is there a difference in cost between cancelling the match night before vs. day off?
[6/20/25, 8:19:54 AM] Shiv Sycl Payments: That's a great question. Our ability to reschedule depends entirely on the facility's cancellation policy. Some facilities require advance notice to cancel without charge, while others bill us regardless of whether the match is played.

When a match is abandoned (for instance, due to weather), we are often charged for the unused booking. If we then schedule a new match, we incur costs for both the original abandoned game and the rescheduled one.

This is precisely why we typically amortize these costs across the entire division. Since any team can be affected by weather-related cancellations, spreading the financial impact is the fairest approach.

[8/1/25, 3:12:40 PM] Kiran Komaravolu SYCL: Dear  SYCL Community- 

We are thrilled to announce a significant step forward in youth cricket for the Seattle region: SYCL and NACC have joined forces under joint governance to form a unified league structure that streamlines the development and organization of competitive cricket across age groups.

Under this new collaboration:

SYCL will oversee all competitions for U11 to U15 age categories.

NACC will continue to host the U17 and above divisions.

This partnership is founded on the principles of transparency, inclusivity, and development, aiming to offer a seamless cricketing pathway from entry-level play through advanced competition.

We’re excited about the opportunities this unification brings—for players, families, and academies alike. By working together, we aim to raise the bar for youth cricket in our region and ensure every child has a clear and supportive path to grow in the sport.

More updates will follow shortly, including details on committee nominations, league calendars, and our upcoming town hall meeting.

Thank you for being part of this exciting new chapter. Let’s play as one!

[8/3/25, 1:56:18 PM] Shiv Sycl Payments: Hello Team Managers,
A hearty welcome to all the new team managers joining SYCL.  I am Shiv and the league treasurer.

I'm writing to outline SYCL’s dues structure for the this year. Hopefully it is a good refresher for the current TMs too. 

Our primary goal is to make the financial process transparent and simpler for you to manage for your teams.

*TL;DR:*
We're have moved to a simple, fixed-cost model. The new costs are:
 * 30-Over Match: $240 per team / match 
 * 40-Over Match: $300 per team / match 

*Our Dues Strategy in Detail*
As a non-profit, our highest priority is keeping the cost for parents as low as possible. In previous years, the post-season billing process was time-consuming, as we had to wait for all the invoices to be cleared, the delay created a lot of overhead for the managers. After analyzing costs from the past four seasons, we realized that the costs followed a very predictable trend. We decided to switch to a static cost model. This gives everyone clear, upfront costs, helps the league operate more efficiently and reduces the overheads for the TMs. 

*League Cost Breakdown*
The league will invoice each team a fixed rate per match:
🏏 30-Over Matches
 * Cost Per Team: $240
🏏 40-Over Matches
 * Cost Per Team: $300

As a league, our target is to keep the per-player cost around $20 (for a 30-over) and $25 (for a 40-over), assuming a 12-player team. You are empowered to calculate the final cost per player based on your actual roster size and team policies.

*Sample Calculation*
A 13A team played 5 T30 matches in a season. They owe 5x240=1,200 for the season. The TMs are empowered to split this cost across teams as they see fit.

*Frequently Asked Questions (FAQ)*
To help you address questions from parents, we've prepared this brief FAQ:
Q1: What if our team has more or fewer than 12 players?
A: The league charges the fixed rate per team. The manager calculates the final per-player cost based on the team's specific roster size and played games. 

Q2: What is the policy if a child misses a game?
A: Policies for individual player absences are handled at the team level by the manager.

Q3: What happens if the league's actual costs are lower than projected?
A: As a non-profit, any surplus is rolled over to help offset costs for the following year, keeping dues low for all families.

Q4: How are rainouts or cancelled matches handled?
A: We are bound by the cancellation policies of the facilities. If the league is charged for a cancelled game, we must pass that cost on to the scheduled teams.

Q5: What is the policy on forfeits?
A: The team that forfeits a scheduled game will be charged the full match fee.

We believe this new model will simplify budget management significantly. Please let me know if you have any questions at all.
Thanks for everything you do for your teams and the league.

[8/8/25, 9:08:13 PM] Anthony Bonthu SYCL: *Fall registration and schedule*
1. Registration is closing tonight 
2. ⁠Schedule will be released by Monday night 
3. ⁠Managers Review and get back for any discrepancies by Wednesday 
4. ⁠Schedule will be locked and will not be changed

*How do you request a schedule exception*
1. We try give exceptions for the below reasons (anything else will not be considered)
2. ⁠If more than 50% of the team traveling to play outside of Seattle 
3. ⁠If more than 50% of the team participating in USA sponsored cricket competitions (hub/regional/national)
4. ⁠*NWCL conflicts will not be considered*
5. ⁠kids traveling for vacation (local or International) will not be considered 

*When to request exceptions*

[8/12/25, 9:34:01 AM] Kiran Komaravolu SYCL: Framework/Suggested Guidelines	All Batters retire after facing 15 balls if the strike rate is less than 60%, continue for another 10 balls if the strike rate is above 60% and batsman retires after 25 balls
	Retired player can come back, highest strike rate player can come back first
	Rotate batting positions of players
	
	
	All Players must bowl atleast 2 overs and no more than 4 overs
	Pick similar skill level bowler as batsman for meaningful contest
	
	Rotate the fielders so that everyone gets a feel for different positions
	Every kid does something, let them play the way they play, let them enjoy the game


Above are guidelines for DivC. Its meant for kids playing with a leather ball for 1st time - no age limits and no promotion/demotion.

[8/18/25, 1:02:45 PM] Kiran Komaravolu SYCL: Field setup is responsibility of home team. Ground should have been ready for play by 3.00 pm.
If delay is caused by home team they lose 1 over for every 5 mins delay caused  (see section 7.4).
Yesterday Bolts were home team - they could lost up to 6 overs and played only for 21 overs.

If delay is unavoidable (e.g. bad weather) - then both teams will lose overs (see unavoidable delays section).
i.e. each team bats for 24 over.

Game *never goes beyond 5 hours* - ground booking was from 3.00 pm to 8.00 pm (yesterday game went until 8.30 pm)

This is good learning for both teams. Since these are pre-season games its fine - you can declare the result by DLS.
During Fall season please keep above points in mind - its the job of the neutral umpire to keep time.

[8/22/25, 8:55:48 PM] Anthony Bonthu SYCL: *Important reminders*
1. Home team is responsible to give POM medal at the end of the game. 
2. ⁠Games must start on time and finish on time. No extension
My suggestion for number of overs (just a suggestion, not a rule based on this summer observation) 
U11B- 25 overs
U11A - 27 overs
U13B - 27 overs
U13A - 30 overs
U15B - 30 overs
U15A - 30 overs
3. Make sure to send neutral umpires. There are heavy penalties for skipping 
4. ⁠Don’t forfeit the game. There are penalties 
5. ⁠Respect players, umpires, parents, spectators and opponents 
6. ⁠Maintain the grounds clean. There are penalties 
7. ⁠For any disputes, escalate to proper channels 
8. ⁠Respect umpires decisions
9. Most importantly, let the kids have fun


All the best to the teams, players, coaches, managers and parents!!! ‎<This message was edited>

This is the first time that a player/manager has willingly violated this rule.
Neither the parent or the manager had sent a request to SYCL.

1. Forfeit the games the player has played.
 Super11 will get the win in the U13A game.
2. $100 fine for both the teams the player has played (Ti2C as well as MSK Tigers)
3. Suspend the player not to play any SYCL games 
for one week as the player disrespected his teammates, opponents, and the game.
 Player will sit out SYCL games for the weekend of Sept 13/14.

[10/19/25, 6:01:54 PM] Tharif Aafzal Seattle Charger Jets Manager: My 2 cents, in a cricket league with multiple divisions (e.g., Division A, Division B, etc.), promotion and relegation are usually decided independently of the finals—based primarily on league standings over the full season, not just who reaches the final. Teams moving up or down are often judged on consistent performance across all matches, not knockout results.
[10/19/25, 6:02:44 PM] Tharif Aafzal Seattle Charger Jets Manager: *Promotion Rules (Typical)*
	•	Top 1 or 2 teams in lower division get promoted to the division above.
	•	Some leagues promote both finalists, regardless of who wins.
	•	Some promote only the league winner (team finishing 1st after points table), and the final is only for the trophy.

*Relegation Rules*
	•	Bottom 1 or 2 teams in the higher division (Div A) get relegated to Div B.
	•	Relegation is strictly based on season-long performance, not finals.

[5/5/26, 9:25:35 AM] ~ Saibaba Talluri: Thanks Raghu for raising this question and Kiran for quick reply with the document to support the logic behind. 

This is BASED on 8 teams in the division - Following the same document logic.  SYCL Group-Based Scheduling Protocol (8-Team Divisions)

1. Rationale
To maintain a strict 6-week season and avoid weather washouts or tournament conflicts, divisions with 7 or 8 teams will utilize a Group-Based Hybrid Format. This ensures all teams play 6 games without the time requirement of a full 8-team round-robin.
Ranking Matches: To ensure all teams complete a 6th game and establish final season standings for promotion/relegation, the remaining teams play cross-bracket matches:
3rd vs. 8th
4th vs. 7th
5th vs. 6th

For 6-Team Divisions: A "best-vs-best" approach is used to ensure more competitive final standings. The remaining teams play:

1st vs. 2nd (Championship/Top Seed Match)
3rd vs. 4th
5th vs. 6th

[5/5/26, 9:45:11 AM] ~ Saibaba Talluri: I fully support the current hybrid mechanism for 8-team divisions; it is an excellent way to ensure all teams get 6 games while effectively identifying the bottom-tier rankings within a tight window.

However, since this specific division  A contains only 6 teams, the "bottom-heavy" logic used for an 8-team bracket is unnecessary. To provide the most equitable experience and establish the most accurate final standings, we should pair teams against their closest statistical neighbors.

For the 6-team format, I personally recommend the following Ranking Matches (and request commitee to review and accept it):

1st vs. 2nd
3rd vs. 4th
5th vs. 6th

This "best-vs-best" pairing ensures that teams are playing for their final rank against their most direct competitors, resulting in a fairer and more competitive conclusion to the season.
